(3) On-policy distillation from MolParser 1.5. (4) DPO on 500 (correct, plausible-wrong) pairs targeting malformed Markush extensions, index tags and chirality. Uni-Parser Bench (overall / chiral / Markush / full): Mobile 0.823/0.732/0.746/0.933; MolParser 1.0 0.800/0.676/0.664/0.953; MolScribe 0.417/0.274/0.168/0.617. BioVista (11,212 instances from 500 papers) overall: Mobile 0.801, MolParser 0.703, MolMiner 0.507, MolScribe 0.455, MolNexTR 0.401, DECIMER 0.298. General VLMs on UniParserBench / BioVista: Qwen3.5-397B 0.272/0.347, Gemma4-31B 0.073/0.118. Throughput: 1,520 molecules/s on an RTX 4090D (FP16); 78 molecules/s on a 14-vCPU CPU. MolGallery is gated and CC-BY-NC-SA.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807 ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/UniParser/MolGallery ; https://huggingface.co/UniParser/MolParser-Mobile-V2
- [OK] **F8** (method) COMO (Apr 2026) adds Minimum Risk Training on molecule-level, non-differentiable rewards to a MolScribe-style model and reports the best exact match on all 10 classic and real benchmarks, using about 4.7x less data than MolParser.
  - details: Loss: interleaved MLE plus MRT. After 5 MLE-only warmup epochs, one MRT step runs every K MLE steps. MRT settings: N=32 multinomial samples at T=0.5, alpha=1.0, lambda=0.1. Reward R = 0.1*valid + 0.5*Sim*valid + 0.4*exact, where Sim is edit distance, Morgan Tanimoto, or a visual Siamese 'MolDiscriminator' score. MRT data: about 83K MolParser-SFT real images (only SMILES needed, no coordinates). MLE data: 1M PubChem plus 652K USPTO, with wildcards remapped to the visible labels. Exact match (MolScribe protocol; UOB tautomer-standardized; corrected CLEF/JPO/UOB from alphaExtractor; 111 WildMol samples removed) for Indigo / ChemDraw / CLEF / JPO / UOB / USPTO / USPTO-10K / Staker / ACS / WildMol-10K: COMO-EditDistance 98.8/96.3/95.0/89.1/98.5/93.0/95.9/87.5/88.2/77.2. MolScribe 97.5/93.8/88.9/76.2/87.9/92.6/96.0/86.9/71.9/66.4. MolParser-Base: CLEF 90.7, JPO 78.9, UOB 91.8, USPTO 93.0, USPTO-10K 94.5, WildMol 76.9. Ablation: the same 83K images used with MLE instead of MRT degraded 5 of 10 benchmarks, so the gain comes from MRT. MRT-only fine-tuning of SwinOCSR gave 2-20x gains, e.g. UOB 49.3 to 98.1 and WildMol 10.6 to 53.4.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546 ; https://huggingface.co/Keylab/COMO ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/Keylab/OCSR-Benchmarks
- [OK] **F9** (method) GTR-CoT / GTR-Mol-VLM (2025, Shanghai AI Lab) fine-tunes Qwen2.5-VL-3B to emit an interleaved atom-bond depth-first graph traversal ('visual chain of thought') before the SMILES. It shows that annotations faithful to the image's abbreviations are decisive.
  - details: Data: GTR-CoT-1.3M = 1M PubChem renders plus 351K USPTO samples, filtered from USPTO-680K by: keeping single-molecule images, OCR-detecting abbreviations, and rewriting the ground-truth graph so abbreviations stay as superatoms. Training: full fine-tuning, 32 A100s, AdamW, peak LR 1.6e-4, effective batch 1024, 3 epochs. MolRec-Bench: MolRec-USPTO (5,423 images) and MolRec-Abb (9,311 images with superatoms from USPTO-10K-abb). Metrics: Gen_SMILES (generated SMILES), Gra_SMILES (SMILES rebuilt from the predicted graph), Graph (graph exact match). Officially released MolScribe/MolNexTR drop below 20% on MolRec-Abb (over 70% on MolRec-USPTO). Retrained on GTR-CoT-1.3M, they improve sharply, but GTR-Mol-VLM is still best. Reported figures (from secondary summaries, not the paper's own table): GTR-Mol-VLM Gen_SMILES about 84.5 on Abb and 91.2 on USPTO; MolScribe official about 20.1/71.8, retrained about 72.2/85.5; GPT-4o about 0.6. Ablations: CoT vs direct SMILES +2.31 Gen_SMILES; traversal vs atoms-then-bonds +2.86/+3.11 Gra_SMILES. On independent MolRecBench-Wild v1 it scored 40.43 SMILES / 35.22 simplified graph, about the same as MolScribe.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07553 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05832
- [OK] **F10** (number) MinerU.Chem (Aug 2026) is the strongest real-literature OCSR reported so far. Its VLM recognizer GTR-VL-1.4.13 natively outputs the CARBON graph and scores 93.02% SMILES EM and 79.66% graph accuracy on revised MolRecBench-Wild.
  - details: Training data has three parts: curated and normalized open-source sets converted to CARBON; human-annotated real-literature crops (MolRecBench-Wild protocol); and synthetic data targeting all 37 MOSAIC difficulty labels (18 visual, 19 chemical). Evaluation: revised MolRecBench-Wild, 5,024 images all with graph labels; only 2,392 have valid SMILES. Subsets: A has few visual and no chemical-semantic difficulty labels; B has more visual labels; C has chemical-semantic labels. SMILES / Graph scores: MinerU.Chem full 93.02/79.66, A 98.28/92.15, B 93.49/80.11, C 70.13/55.42. GPT-5.6-Sol 74.87/32.19. Gemini-3.5-flash-thinking 66.85/36.41. Claude-opus-4-8 65.47/14.05. InternVL3.5 39.80/3.05. MolNexTR 62.50, MolScribe 62.29, Mathpix 47.32, DECIMER v2.2 41.43, MolGrapher 34.78, ChemDFM-X 19.06, OCSU 11.41, ChemVLM 8.03 (SMILES only). Detection (MolYOLO) on MolDet-33k-test: P/R@IoU0.5 = 0.98/0.98. The system also links compound identifiers (e.g. '1a') and parses reactions. Stated gap: no automatic Markush or R-group substitution-table expansion yet. Released as an online service; weights not stated as released.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525 ; https://mineru.net/OpenSourceTools/Extractor
- [FIXED] **F11** (number) MolRecBench-Wild (CVPR 2026 Findings) is the first difficulty-annotated real-paper OCSR benchmark. In its first release no open model exceeded about 41% SMILES exact match, and frontier VLMs scored lower.
  - details: About 820 CC-BY papers from 7 chemistry journals were cropped with DocLayout-YOLO plus an in-house detector: 7,000 candidates, 5,029 selected (v1). The HF release dated 2026-08-19 has 5,024 images from 818 articles. MOSAIC labels: 18 visual (e.g. decorated text or bonds, blur, arrows/boxes/text, colored backgrounds, bond crossing, R drawn as a pattern, short or long or thick or thin bonds, numbered atoms, incomplete molecule, large molecule). 19 chemical (e.g. equal-width chiral bonds, charges, dashed, wavy and hash bonds, lone pairs, ionic and coordination bonds, R on aromatic ring with uncertain position, abbreviations, valence symbols, polymers, multi-group, transition states). 93.29% of samples carry at least one label and 42% carry labels in both dimensions. Annotation: 47 chemistry annotators, 152 person-days, at least 2 QC rounds, Ketcher. v1 results (SMILES / simplified graph / graph): MolScribe 41.05/34.47, MolNexTR 40.90/34.42, GTR-Mol-VLM 40.43/35.22, MolGrapher 20.33/22.81, DECIMERv2.2 22.84, OCSU 6.06. Gemini 2.5 Pro 30.06/15.67/13.04; GPT-5 19.68/10.00/8.19; InternVL3.5-241B 25.60/6.88/3.08; GPT-4o 7.94/3.74/2.94; ChemDFM-X 9.75; ChemVLM 4.79; Mathpix 27.88. Scores always rank SMILES > simplified graph > graph. The dataset card declares no license.
  - CORRECTION: MolRecBench-Wild v1 (arXiv May 2026) has 5,029 images from about 820 CC-BY papers in 7 journals. The 2026-08-19 HF release has 5,024 images from 818 articles. The HF card cites it as 'CVPR 2026'; 'Findings' could not be verified. The SMILES > simplified graph > graph ordering does not always hold: MolGrapher scores 20.33 SMILES vs 22.81 simplified graph.
  - checker note: Everything else matches. 7,000 candidates, about 6,548 after screening, 5,029 selected. 18 visual + 19 chemical labels; 93.29% carry at least one label and 42% carry both. 47 annotators, 152 person-days, ≥2 QC rounds, Ketcher. All v1 scores match Table 2, and the card declares no license. Also note that in v1 a non-reasoning VLM (InternVL3.5, 25.60) beat several reasoning models on SMILES.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05832 ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/opendatalab/MolRecBench-Wild
- [FIXED] **F12** (existence) The classic OCSR benchmarks come almost entirely from patents or catalogs, have narrow difficulty coverage, and differ in size and resolution.
  - details: From MolRecBench-Wild Table 1 and other papers, with image counts and mean size where known: Staker 49,557-50,000 at 256x256, SMILES ground truth. UOB (Maybridge catalog, scanned) 5,740, about 762x412, no stereo. USPTO 5,719 (5,638 in one count), about 688x437, 20.5% chiral. CLEF-2012 992 (906 in one count), 32.7% chiral. JPO 450, Japanese-patent conventions, poor quality, 0% chiral. ACS 331 journal images (MolScribe). USPTO-30K (clean, abb, large). MolMole Patents 2,469 (from MolMole_Patent300, 300 patent pages). WildMol-10K and WildMol-10K-M (MolParser). Synthetic Indigo and ChemDraw sets of 5,719 each. All classic sets have fewer than 10 visual and fewer than 3 chemical MOSAIC difficulty labels. Chirality shares are from the MolSeek-OCR benchmark table. Classic sets also contain near-duplicate batches from the same patent (noted by MolGrapher).
  - CORRECTION: In the MolSeek-OCR benchmark table the chiral shares are USPTO 20.2%, CLEF 32.7%, JPO 0%, UOB 0%, Staker 17.3%, ACS 19.3%, Indigo/ChemDraw 20.2%. The 20.5% USPTO figure is MolGrapher's 'stereo-chemistry proportion'; that table also gives CLEF 33.9% and JPO 1.0%.
  - checker note: Image counts and sizes match MolRecBench-Wild Table 1: Staker 49,557 at 256x256; UOB 5,740 at 762x412; USPTO 5,638 at 688x437; CLEF 906; MolMole Patents 2,469; all classic sets have <10 visual and <3 chemical labels. MolGrapher does note near-duplicate batches from the same patent. Pinned copies used by COMO (HF) have JPO 449 and USPTO-10K 9,999.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05832 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03476 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.12234 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.14311
- [OK] **F13** (method) Exact-match definitions are not standardized; the matching rule alone can move the same model on the same benchmark by about 6 points.
  - details: Definitions in use: MolScribe and COMO use canonical SMILES with tetrahedral chirality kept, cis/trans ignored and R mapped to [d*]. COMO additionally tautomer-standardizes UOB. MolGrapher uses InChIKey equality with stereo removed and Markush excluded. MarkushGrapher uses InChIKey of the non-Markush part plus exact m/Sg sections, stereo ignored. MolMole reports both InChIKey and SMILES accuracy. MolRecBench-Wild separates SMILES, simplified-graph and full-graph tracks. ChemVLM, ChemVLR and the hand-drawn studies count Tanimoto=1.0 on fingerprints (ECFP6/Morgan), which can treat stereoisomers as identical. MolSight-2607 uses an 'adaptive' rule that checks stereo only when the ground truth has stereo. Example on USPTO-5719 (Glyph README): MolScribe 88.4 canonical with all stereo, 92.6 chirality-kept, 94.6 graph-only; OCSRGlyph 93.8/93.9/96.2. COMO notes that MolParser's WildMol E-SMILES-to-SMILES scoring protocol is undocumented.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.14311 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.12234 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16096 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03777 ; https://github.com/EdisonScientific/glyph ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01982 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06685 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01743
- [OK] **F14** (lesson) Public OCSR benchmarks contain known label errors and change between versions, so scores are only comparable on pinned, corrected versions.
  - details: COMO uses manually corrected CLEF, JPO and UOB from alphaExtractor (Xiong et al., Sci China Life Sci 2024). It reports pervasive tautomer mislabels in UOB. In WildMol-10K, groups like 'Boc' are labeled as '*' wildcards plus an extension; COMO expanded these and dropped 111 samples for expansion failures or index conflicts. MolParser states the classic sets have 'systematic biases and annotation noise'. MolRecBench-Wild changed from 5,029 samples (v1 arXiv) to 5,024 (2026-08-19 release). The same models' SMILES scores rose substantially between the v1 paper and the revised version used by MinerU.Chem: MolScribe 41.05 to 62.29, DECIMER 22.84 to 41.43. MarkushGrapher-2 says IP5-M has 1,000 structures, while the HF test config has 878 rows, and Glyph re-scored MarkushGrapher-2 at 53.2 vs its published 53.7.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11098 ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/opendatalab/MolRecBench-Wild ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525 ; https://github.com/EdisonScientific/glyph ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/docling-project/MarkushGrapher-2-Datasets
- [FIXED] **F15** (number) Zero-shot general VLMs were near zero at OCSR through 2025. Frontier reasoning VLMs in 2026 reach 65-75% SMILES exact match on real crops but remain poor at graph-level fidelity, Markush structures and open-weight scale.
  - details: Image-to-SMILES ACC (MolSight-2607 Table 1, ChemMLLM benchmark): GPT-4o 0.01, Qwen-VL-7B 0.0, InternVL-20B 0.0. Tani@1.0 on MMChemOCR / img2smiles (ChemVLR Table 3): Gemini-3-Flash 61.2/63.8, GPT-5-mini 5.7/5.1, GPT-4o 3.4/0.1, Qwen2.5-VL-7B 0.4/0.0, Qwen3-VL-8B 10.2/1.3, InternVL3.5-8B 59.1/4.4. DeepSeek-OCR-2 zero-shot: 0.0-0.2% on all OCSR sets. MolRecBench-Wild v1: GPT-5 19.68, Gemini 2.5 Pro 30.06. Revised MolRecBench-Wild (SMILES / graph): GPT-5.6-Sol 74.87/32.19, Gemini-3.5-flash-thinking 66.85/36.41, Claude-opus-4-8 65.47/14.05. Markush CXSMILES EM on M2S: GPT-4o 4%, GPT-5 3%, DeepSeek-OCR 0%. Open models on UniParserBench: Qwen3.5-397B 0.272, Gemma4-31B 0.073. Reasoning ('thinking') models consistently outperform non-reasoning VLMs.
  - CORRECTION: General VLMs were near zero on clean rendered OCSR in 2024-2025. Examples: ChemMLLM benchmark ACC GPT-4o 0.01, Qwen-VL-7B 0.0; DeepSeek-OCR-2 0.0-0.2%; M2S CXSMILES GPT-4o 4, GPT-5 3, DeepSeek-OCR 0. But some 2025 models were already well above zero: Gemini 2.5 Pro 30.06 and InternVL3.5-241B 25.60 SMILES on MolRecBench-Wild v1; InternVL3.5-8B 59.1 and Gemini-3-Flash 61.2 Tani@1.0 on MMChemOCR. Reasoning models do not consistently beat non-reasoning ones. InternVL3.5 (25.60 v1; 39.80 revised) beats GPT-5 (19.68), Seed1.6-Thinking (15.60/24.83), Intern-S1 (18.98/30.02) and GLM-4.5V on SMILES.
  - checker note: Individual numbers all verified; only the two generalizations are wrong. The 2026 frontier figures match MinerU.Chem Table 4.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01982 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06685 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03476 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05832 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16096 ; https://github.com/DS4SD/MarkushGrapher ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807
- [OK] **F16** (lesson) Naively fine-tuning a document-OCR VLM to emit SMILES (MolSeek-OCR, from DeepSeek-OCR-2, Apr 2026) reaches only DECIMER-level accuracy, below MolScribe. Direct full SFT and GSPO/ReFT post-training failed.
  - details: Recipe: direct full-parameter SFT diverged, so the authors used two stages. Stage 1: LoRA r=64, alpha=64 on attention, FFN and vision-language projector; lr 2e-4; 3,000 steps; 96k-192k samples. Stage 2: selective full fine-tuning with the visual tokenizer and embeddings frozen and split learning rates (vision 5e-6, language 2e-5); 2,500 steps; 800k samples (300k MolScribe-style plus 300k ChemDraw-style PubChem renders plus 200k USPTO-MOL). Exact match for Indigo / ChemDraw / CLEF / UOB / USPTO / Staker / ACS: MolSeek-OCR 74.3/72.2/63.3/72.6/61.0/50.5/29.9 vs MolScribe 97.5/93.8/88.9/87.9/92.6/86.9/71.9. GSPO with a chemistry reward (validity, sequence, graph and stereo terms) raised graph-level accuracy slightly but lowered SMILES exact match, so it was dropped.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03476 ; https://github.com/HaCTang/MolSeek-OCR
- [UNVERIFIED] **F17** (lesson) A 2026 controlled study of 21 fine-tuned VLM recognizers finds that the fraction of real labeled training images, not more synthetic data, closes the synthetic-to-real gap. Whether vision-tower LoRA helps depends on the base model.
  - details: Qwen2.5-VL-7B fine-tuned on synthetic data scores above 91% on synthetic renders but under 16% on real benchmarks. ACS exact match: 0.15 with no real data, 0.37 with about 10% real, 0.46 with about 50% real. Vision-tower LoRA: Qwen +0.00 (p=1.00), InternVL3-8B +22.8 to +34.6 pts, GLM-4.1V-9B +1.0 to +9.6. Best configuration: 0.96 clean renders, 0.49 ACS, 0.65 CLEF-IP, 0.84 UOB, 0.76 USPTO. Differences between base models are largest with no real data and shrink as real data is added. These figures come from search-engine summaries of the arXiv page, not a direct read of the paper.
  - checker note: arXiv 2608.09100 is not indexed on HF Papers. arXiv itself was unreachable from this environment and the web-search budget was exhausted, so neither the paper's existence nor any figure (Qwen 91%→<16%, ACS 0.15/0.37/0.46, vision-LoRA effects) could be checked. The analyst also flagged these as search-summary figures.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09100
- [UNVERIFIED] **F18** (method) Agreement among several architecturally different recognizers is a strong label-free reliability signal for OCSR. Re-rendering a prediction and comparing pixels is barely better than chance.
  - details: VERDICT (arXiv 2608.22183, Aug 2026) tested on 263 ACS depictions with verified ground truth. AUROC for flagging errors: pixel-space re-render 0.547; agreement of 4 recognizers, compared by molecular identity, 0.916. The same ordering holds on CLEF-IP, UOB and USPTO. A substance filter removes false agreement on wildcard or R-group fragments. Operating points: precision 0.888 at 81.7% coverage, 0.985 at 52.1% coverage, about 3.5 s per image. Run on PubMed Central OA, it produced 6,146 labels for 4,833 molecules; a chemist audit of 400 labels found precision 0.995 (3-of-4 agree) and 0.958 (2-of-4). Consistent with MolParser's ensemble Tanimoto confidence and MolGallery's 3-model unanimity filter. Figures come from search summaries, not a direct read.
  - checker note: VERDICT (arXiv 2608.22183) could not be found on HF Papers, and arXiv was unreachable, so AUROC 0.547 vs 0.916, the operating points and the PMC audit are unverified. The supporting context is verified: MolParser's 5-fold Tanimoto confidence and MolGallery's 3-model unanimity filter.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22183 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11098
- [OK] **F19** (method) MolSight (AAAI 2026, HUST) was the first OCSR system to use GRPO post-training, mainly for stereochemistry. Its gains on clean patents did not carry over to in-the-wild crops.
  - details: Three stages: (1) SMILES pretraining on MolParser-7M; (2) multi-granularity SFT on PubChem-1M plus USPTO-680K, with SMILES, edge and optional coordinate heads; (3) GRPO with LoRA for 2 epochs on Stereo-200k (200,000 stereoisomeric molecules); evaluated on Stereo-2k. GRPO reward reportedly combines Tanimoto and stereo terms (search summary). USPTO accuracy from the README: base 91.2, coord 91.1, stereo (RL) 90.3, extra 92.0. COMO's independent evaluation of MolSight-extra: Indigo 96.3, USPTO 93.2, UOB 95.6, Staker 83.4, ACS 72.9, but only 9.0 on WildMol-10K, which COMO attributes to frequent substituent abbreviations plus the missing coordinate prediction. Apache-2.0.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17300 ; https://github.com/hustvl/MolSight ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546 ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/Robert-zwr/Stereo-200k
- [OK] **F20** (method) For RL on VLM-based molecule recognition, a binary 'structural identity' reward (Tanimoto exactly 1.0) beat both exact-string and dense-Tanimoto rewards. RL alone without SFT does nothing.
  - details: ChemVLR (Apr 2026) on Qwen3-VL-8B / Qwen2.5-VL-7B: chemistry continued pretraining, then SFT on reasoning plus instruction data (including molecule-to-IUPAC), then DAPO RL. RL data: 4 rollouts per sample from the SFT model; only samples with divergent outcomes kept (100k, about 25k per task). Rewards: binary accuracy plus a <think>/<answer> format reward. Img2smiles Tani@1.0 by reward: Struct-ID 92.7, exact string 90.8, dense Tanimoto 90.2. By stage: SFT-only 81.5, RL-only 2.1, CPT+SFT 84.7, full 92.7. Dropping SFT data: without instruction data 68.4, without reasoning 76.4, without IUPAC 80.8 (vs 84.7). Benchmarks: ChemVLR-8B MMChemOCR Tani@1.0 84.6; TinyChemVL 77.4; ChemDFM-X 36.5; ChemVLM-8B 57.7. These are clean, largely rendered benchmarks scored with Tanimoto=1.0, which is weaker than canonical-SMILES EM.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06685
- [OK] **F21** (number) MolSight-2607 (a different paper from F19, July 2026) is a graph-aware Qwen3-VL VLM. It reports 0.81-0.83 image-to-SMILES ACC on a clean benchmark but needs an image-derived SVG as a second input, which limits use on scanned documents.
  - details: A Molecular Topology Module predicts a bond-adjacency matrix over vision tokens, supervised by adjacency parsed from SVG. A Molecular Grounding Module cross-attends vision tokens to SVG text tokens. Base model: Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct. Stage 2 freezes the vision encoder and adapter and LoRA-tunes the LLM. Image-to-SMILES on the ChemMLLM benchmark (Avg Tanimoto / ACC / Valid): MolSight-8B 0.998/0.81/99.7%; MolSight-32B 0.999/0.83; DECIMER 0.97/0.78; MolScribe 0.98/0.66; ChemMLLM-34B 0.87/0.56; ChemVLM-8B 0.55/0.11; GPT-4o 0.29/0.01. Dominant error types: stereo errors and near-miss local edits. Also covers descriptor estimation (MW, LogP, TPSA, QED, etc.) and bioactivity prediction from images.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01982
- [OK] **F22** (number) Chemistry-generalist VLMs trained mostly on clean renders (OCSU/Mol-VL, ChemVLM, ChemDFM-X, ChemMLLM) perform poorly on real-literature OCSR.
  - details: OCSU (2025): Vis-CheBI20 has 29.7K RDKit-rendered diagrams and 117.7K image-text pairs covering functional groups, descriptions, IUPAC names and SMILES. Mol-VL-7B is full-SFT from Qwen2-VL. DoubleCheck is a MolScribe variant with attentive re-look at ambiguous atoms. Real-world SMILES exact match: OCSU 6.06% (MolRecBench-Wild v1) and 11.41% (revised). ChemVLM 4.79 and 8.03. ChemDFM-X 9.75 and 19.06. ChemVLM authors acknowledge their model trails DECIMER and MolScribe on their own ChemOCR set (1,000 pairs from MolScribe data).
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.15415 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07246 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05832 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525
- [OK] **F23** (method) MolMole (LG AI Research, 2025) shows that detection-based OCSR outputting layout-preserving MOLfiles works well page-to-structure, handling polymers and wavy bonds and avoiding generative hallucination.
  - details: Components: ViDetect (DINO-DETR molecule detector), ViReact (RxnScribe-style page-level reaction parser), ViMore (detects atom regions and bond types, builds a MOLfile with coordinates, outputs low/medium/high confidence). Page-level benchmark: 550 pages (patents plus articles), 3,897 molecules, 1,022 reactions. A detection plus OCSR true positive requires IoU ≥ 0.5 and an identical SMILES. Detection AP/AR/F1: patents 0.914/0.938/0.926, articles 0.928/0.949/0.938. ViMore SMILES accuracy with ground-truth boxes: 90% patents, 88% articles. End-to-end F1: patents 0.891, articles 0.868. Public-benchmark InChIKey accuracy: CLEF 85.3, JPO 81.5, USPTO 93.8 (best on 3 of 4). Generative models showed repeated-token and chemical-prior hallucinations. Weights are proprietary.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03777 ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/doxa-friend/MolMole_Patent300
- [OK] **F24** (number) MarkushGrapher (CVPR 2025, IBM) defined multimodal Markush recognition: it encodes the image plus OCR text and layout and outputs an optimized CXSMILES plus a substituent table. Absolute accuracy on real patents stayed low.
  - details: Architecture: a UDOP-style vision-text-layout encoder plus a MolScribe OCSR encoder, concatenated and fed to a T5-style text decoder. The 'optimized CXSMILES' moves R-groups inline as special atoms, appends <i>index</i> per atom and keeps m (position variation) and Sg (frequency variation) sections. Synthetic training data: PubChem SMILES augmented to CXSMILES, rendered with CDK under random styles; textual R-group definitions from patent-derived templates, partly LLM-paraphrased. Benchmarks: M2S (103 real USPTO/EPO/WIPO crops with substituent tables), USPTO-Markush (74), MarkushGrapher-Synthetic (1,000). Metrics: CXSMILES EM requires the InChIKey of the core to match and correct R, m and Sg sections, stereo ignored; Tanimoto; table EM and F1; Markush EM needs both CXSMILES and table exact. Results: M2S CXSMILES EM 38 (MolScribe 21, DECIMER 3, GPT-4o 4, Pixtral and Llama-3.2-V 0), table EM 29 / F1 65 (GPT-4o 19/49), Markush EM 10. USPTO-Markush CXSMILES EM 32 (MolScribe 7, though MolScribe's Tanimoto was higher at 97 vs 74). Synthetic: 65/84/57.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16096 ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/docling-project/MarkushGrapher-Datasets
- [FIXED] **F25** (number) MarkushGrapher-2 (CVPR 2026) makes Markush recognition end-to-end with a built-in ChemicalOCR module, adds the IP5-M real benchmark, and raises CXSMILES accuracy to 53-56%.
  - details: Two-phase training: (1) adapt the decoder to pretrained OCSR features using 243k MolScribe USPTO pairs; (2) fuse with the vision-text-layout encoder using 235k synthetic CXSMILES (MarkushGenerator), 91k MolParser samples converted to CXSMILES, and 54k USPTO-MOL-M samples auto-extracted from 2010-2025 USPTO MOL files. Initialized from UDOP; 831M parameters (744M trainable). CXSMILES accuracy on M2S / USPTO-M / WildMol-M / IP5-M: MarkushGrapher-2 56/55/48.0/53.7; MolParser-Base 39/30/38.1/47.7; MolScribe 21/7/28.1/22.3; MarkushGrapher-1 38/32; GPT-5 3 on M2S; DeepSeek-OCR 0/0/1.9/0.0. IP5-M: about 1,000 manually annotated Markush structures from the USPTO, JPO, KIPO, CNIPA and EPO offices (HF test config shows 878 rows). On standard OCSR (WildMol/JPO/UOB/USPTO) it scores 68.4/71.0/96.6/89.8, competitive but behind MolParser on WildMol. MIT license; weights on HF (docling-project/MarkushGrapher-2, ChemicalOCR). Numbers come from a WebFetch summary of the GitHub README.
  - CORRECTION: The GitHub repo is MIT-licensed, but the released weights (HF docling-project/MarkushGrapher-2, architecture 'udop') are Apache-2.0.
  - checker note: All other details match the README: CVPR 2026, arXiv 2603.28550, 831M/744M params, 243k/235k/91k/54k training data. CXSMILES 56/55/48.0/53.7 vs MolParser-Base 39/30/38.1/47.7, MolScribe 21/7/28.1/22.3, GPT-5 3, DeepSeek-OCR 0/0/1.9/0.0. Standard OCSR 68.4/71.0/96.6/89.8. The README describes IP5-M as about 1,000 structures while the HF test config has 878 rows.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28550 ; https://github.com/DS4SD/MarkushGrapher ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/docling-project/MarkushGrapher-2-Datasets ; https://huggingface.co/docling-project/MarkushGrapher-2
- [OK] **F26** (number) MarkushGlyph (Jul 2026, Edison Scientific) shows that a small VLM (Qwen3.5-2B-Base plus LoRA SFT) emitting cxsmiles_opt directly from a crop beats the multi-stage MarkushGrapher-2. Majority voting over 8 samples adds 1-4 points.
  - details: MarkushGlyph: LoRA rank 128, alpha 128, on Qwen/Qwen3.5-2B-Base. Training index of 263,158 rows (MarkushGrapher-1 synthetic, MarkushGrapher-2 structure-only, MolParser), with 5% ordinary OCSR data mixed in. Accuracy with the official MarkushGrapher-2 scorer, unparseable outputs counted wrong, on IP5-M (878) / M2S (103) / USPTO-Markush (74): greedy 58.2/61.2/59.5; MV@8 60.6/62.1/63.5; MarkushGrapher-2 53.2 (re-scored)/56.0/55.0; MolParser-Base 47.7/39.0/30.0. Stricter parsed-graph equality on IP5-M greedy: 52.1. OCSRGlyph (Swin-B/384 plus 6-layer decoder) on USPTO-5719: canonical with all stereo 93.8, chirality-kept 93.9, graph 96.2. Its training mix is PubChem-1M, USPTO-680K, Stereo-200K x2 and adjacent-ring x2; the gain is attributed to oversampling chiral molecules. Apache-2.0. Figures come from WebFetch of the GitHub README and search summaries.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28532 ; https://github.com/EdisonScientific/glyph
- [OK] **F27** (method) Representation choice is the central design decision for generative OCSR. SMILES is compact but loses layout, Markush and non-standard semantics; E-SMILES, CXSMILES and CARBON extend it at different cost.
  - details: SMILES: best accuracy among string forms for image-to-string transformers (DECIMER study); SELFIES guarantees validity at lower accuracy; InChI is unsuitable. Canonicalization makes multiple valid strings per graph a scoring issue. E-SMILES (MolParser): 'SMILES<sep>EXT' with <a>, <r>, <c>, <dum>; RDKit-compatible core. Cannot express dashed abstract rings, coordination bonds, pattern-drawn Markush groups or repeated multi-atom segments. CXSMILES (ChemAxon): m sections (position variation), Sg (frequency variation), attachment labels; MarkushGrapher 'optimizes' it with inline R atoms and atom-index tokens. CARBON (MolRecBench-Wild / MinerU.Chem): atom-centric JSON-like graph with image coordinates, superatoms, non-standard bonds (dashed dative, bold, dashed double), oxidation states, radicals, isotopes, non-integer charges and repeat brackets. It has atom-centric (training) and attribute-centric (analysis) forms and exports to MOLfile/SMILES. MOLfile is explicit but verbose and cannot hold non-standard bonds. Graph traversal as CoT (GTR) interleaves atoms and bonds before the SMILES. Only 2,392 of 5,024 revised MolRecBench-Wild samples have a valid SMILES at all.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11098 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16096 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05832 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525 ; https://pubs.rsc.org/en/content/articlelanding/2022/dd/d1dd00013f ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07553
- [OK] **F28** (lesson) Coordinate-grounded graph outputs and SMILES-caption outputs trade interpretability and stereo handling against annotation cost and scalability. The 2026 evidence is split by team.
  - details: Arguments for graph or coordinate outputs: MolScribe derives chirality from coordinates and makes verification 2x faster (20 s vs 39 s per molecule). MolMole's detection-based MOLfiles avoid hallucination and handle polymers and wavy bonds. COMO attributes MolSight's WildMol collapse (9.0%) to the lack of coordinates. MinerU.Chem's CARBON graph VLM is the 2026 top scorer (93.0 SMILES / 79.7 graph). GTR's traversal CoT beats atoms-then-bonds by about 3 points. Arguments for end-to-end strings: Uni-Parser (DP Tech) reports graph methods need more than 20x annotation effort and have lower ceilings, and MolParser and MolParser-Mobile reach top WildMol and BioVista scores with E-SMILES captions. MolSight's coordinate head gave no gain (91.1 vs 91.2 on USPTO). A pure SMILES-generation VLM (MolSeek-OCR) trails MolScribe by about 30 points on USPTO.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.14311 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03777 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07553 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15098 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807 ; https://github.com/hustvl/MolSight ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03476
- [OK] **F29** (lesson) Abbreviations and superatoms (Ph, Boc, OMe, Ts, R-groups) are a dominant failure source, and most of the damage comes from labels that do not match what is drawn rather than from model capacity.
  - details: MolScribe and MolNexTR official checkpoints drop below 20% on MolRec-Abb (9,311 USPTO images with superatoms) because their training images show abbreviations while the labels are fully expanded. Retraining on GTR's corrected superatom labels lifts them to about 70%+. Approaches: MolScribe predicts superatom text character by character and expands it greedily (handles COOH vs CO2H); MolGrapher uses a 'superatom' node class plus PP-OCR and a lookup table; COMO remaps '*' placeholders in USPTO MOL files to the visible labels (R, X, Me), recovering 652K samples; MolParser keeps them in <a> tags; CARBON keeps superatoms and expands them only at export. Observed errors: DECIMER reading 'Ph' as 'Pb' (MolRecBench-Wild case study); Mathpix collapsing numbered R-groups and dropping a P atom. WildMol labels encode Boc as a wildcard, which penalizes correct expansions.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07553 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.14311 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.12234 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05832
- [OK] **F30** (lesson) Stereochemistry (wedge, hash, wavy and bold bonds) remains the top residual error for end-to-end models. Effective fixes are explicit geometry, oversampling chiral molecules, and stereo-aware RL or DPO.
  - details: Uni-Parser names chirality as MolParser 1.5's main open challenge. MolParser-Mobile's chiral-subset accuracy is 0.732 (Uni-Parser Bench) and 0.653 (BioVista), versus 0.823 and 0.801 overall; its DPO pairs target hard chirality. COMO uses 7 bond classes including solid and dashed wedge, sets stereo from RDKit rules on predicted coordinates, and augments by randomly swapping wedge bonds for wavy bonds. OCSRGlyph doubles Stereo-200K and adjacent-ring data in its training mix. MolSight runs GRPO on Stereo-200k. In MolSight-2607 error analysis, stereo errors (e.g. a spurious '@') plus near-miss local edits dominate. MolRecBench-Wild shows achiral molecules drawn with decorative wedges, where good models must not emit chirality. Benchmarks differ on cis/trans: MolScribe and COMO ignore it, Glyph's canonical metric requires it.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15098 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546 ; https://github.com/EdisonScientific/glyph ; https://github.com/hustvl/MolSight ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01982 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05832
- [OK] **F31** (lesson) Other documented real-document failure modes are low resolution, very large molecules, foreign-script or overlapping text, multiple or overlapping molecules per crop, charges, and joint visual-plus-chemical difficulty.
  - details: Low resolution: Staker (256x256) gives MolGrapher 0.0, OSRA 0.0, MolVec 0.8, SwinOCSR 0.2, versus MolScribe 86.9 and COMO 87.5. Large molecules: on USPTO-10K-L (>70 atoms) MolGrapher scores 31.4 and Img2Mol 0.0; Img2Mol degrades past about 40 atoms. JPO Japanese characters get recognized as extra fragments; MolScribe keeps only the largest fragment. MolParser fails on overlapping large molecules and charged molecules. GTR-CoT filtered multi-molecule images out of its training data. COMO adds random Japanese/Chinese text as augmentation and pads images to preserve aspect ratio. MinerU.Chem accuracy falls from 98.28/92.15 (subset A) to 93.49/80.11 (B, more visual noise) to 70.13/55.42 (C, chemical semantics); GTR-Mol-VLM falls sharply when visual and chemical challenges co-occur. MolScribe on perturbed Staker_p scores 65.0 vs 86.9 clean.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.12234 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.14311 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11098 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05832 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03476
- [OK] **F32** (number) Hand-drawn OCSR is still weak: the best reported Tanimoto=1 accuracy on the DECIMER hand-drawn test split is about 0.63.
  - details: DECIMER hand-drawn dataset: 5,088 images. Oldenhof et al. (2404.01743) split it into 4,070 train/val and 1,018 test, plus a 614-image ChemPix test set. Accuracy is identical ECFP6 (Tanimoto=1) on hand-drawn / ChemPix: DECIMER v2.2.0 0.295/0.05; DECIMER hand-drawn fine-tuned 0.622/0.508; MolScribe 0.102/0.269; Img2Mol 0.084/0.015; ChemGrapher 0.002/0.187; OSRA 0.006/0.047; AtomLenz+EditKT* (weakly supervised atom-level detector with only SMILES supervision) 0.338/0.484; ChemExpert ensemble 0.635/0.518. A 2024 DECIMER paper (J Cheminf 16:78) trained on RanDepict synthetic hand-drawn styles. MolParser added some manually annotated hand-drawn molecules to SFT.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01743 ; https://github.com/Kohulan/DECIMER-Image_Transformer ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11098
- [OK] **F33** (number) Molecule detection and identifier association are mostly solved at the crop level, with detection accuracy at about 0.98-0.99 mAP50.
  - details: MolDetv2 (YOLO11n, 2.6M params): General-n mAP50 0.9872, mAP50:95 0.8776 at 1.5 ms on a T4 (MolDet-Bench-General, 799 images); Doc-n 960px mAP50 0.9936 at 3.1 ms (MolDet-Bench-Doc, 447 pages, 2,178 instances). Uni-Parser-LD reaches mAP50:95 0.9679 at 10.1 ms. MolYOLO (MinerU.Chem/RxnCaption): P/R 0.98/0.98 on MolDet-33k-test, vs MolDetect 0.84/0.77 and YoDe 0.89/0.75. MolMole ViDetect F1 is 0.926 on patents and 0.938 on articles. Identifier linking ('1a' to structure) is done by Uni-Parser-LD's group layout, MinerU.Chem's Mid-Mapper and RxnID's identifier visual prompts. Errors at this stage, such as truncated boxes or merged molecules, propagate into OCSR errors.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03777 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15098 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15011
- [UNVERIFIED] **F34** (existence) End-to-end patent pipelines that combine OCSR, identifier linking and bioactivity table extraction exist and report about 90% task accuracy, but full SAR enumeration from Markush scaffolds plus R-group tables remains unsolved.
  - details: BioChemInsight v2 (arXiv 2504.10525): DECIMER Segmentation, MolNexTR, GLM-4.5V for compound-ID association, PaddleOCR plus GLM-4.6 for bioactivity extraction and unit normalization. Average above 90% across structure recognition, bioactivity extraction and ID association on 181 patents covering 15 targets. Patent chemical space found to be largely complementary to ChEMBL. Figures from a search summary. MinerU.Chem explicitly does not yet expand Markush structures or R-group substitution tables, and calls this future work for molecule-property dataset construction. MarkushGrapher outputs substituent tables but table exact match is only 29% on M2S. BioVista (BioMiner) is a 500-paper protein-ligand bioactivity benchmark, also used for OCSR evaluation (11,212 molecular instances).
  - checker note: Partly verified. BioChemInsight exists (MIT) and uses DECIMER Segmentation, MolNexTR, GLM-4.5V and PaddleOCR. Its README reports no accuracy figures and does not mention GLM-4.6 or ChEMBL. The '>90% across tasks on 181 patents / 15 targets' figure could not be checked, since arXiv 2504.10525 is unreachable and not on HF Papers. Verified: MinerU.Chem does not yet expand Markush or R-group tables; MarkushGrapher table EM is 29% on M2S; BioVista has 500 papers and 11,212 instances.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10525 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16096 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807 ; https://github.com/dahuilangda/BioChemInsight
- [OK] **F35** (number) MolNexTR (J Cheminf 2024) is a MolScribe-style image-to-graph model that matches MolScribe on patents but not on Markush-heavy real crops.
  - details: Design: MolScribe-like graph generation with multi-scale ConvNext and ViT encoders, described in MolRecBench-Wild's related work. USPTO-5719 chirality-kept EM 93.8 (Glyph table). MolRecBench-Wild v1 SMILES 40.90 / simplified graph 34.42; revised SMILES 62.50 (A 76.78, B 52.11, C 34.56). BioVista overall 0.401, Markush 0.045, chiral 0.419. MolRec-Abb Gen_SMILES below 20% with the official checkpoint, about 70%+ after retraining on GTR-CoT-1.3M. Its own paper's per-benchmark table (arXiv 2403.03691) could not be retrieved here.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03691 ; https://github.com/EdisonScientific/glyph ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05832 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07553
- [OK] **F36** (lesson) Reinforcement learning with verifiable, structure-level rewards is now standard in chemistry-diagram VLMs, and its effect depends on the reward matching the evaluation's equivalence notion.
  - details: Positive results: COMO MRT (exact plus validity plus similarity, MolScribe-type model): JPO +12.2 and ACS +14.5 pts over MolScribe. ChemVLR DAPO with Tanimoto=1 reward: +8 pts Tani@1.0 over CPT+SFT. RxnID's Re3-DAPO (Mar 2026) directly optimizes reaction-level metrics with verifiable rewards and gains consistently over SFT for reaction diagram parsing. MolSight GRPO on Stereo-200k. MolParser-Mobile uses on-policy distillation plus a 500-pair DPO calibration. Negative result: MolSeek-OCR's GSPO with a mixed reward lowered SMILES EM while slightly raising graph accuracy. ChemVLR found RL without SFT useless (2.1) and needed difficulty filtering (keep samples whose 4 rollouts disagree) to avoid zero-advantage groups.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06685 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15011 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17300 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03476 ; https://github.com/hustvl/MolSight
- [UNVERIFIED] **F37** (lesson) Independent comparisons find no single OCSR tool dominates across image categories; routing images to the best tool by type, or ensembling, helps.
  - details: Krasnov et al., Digital Discovery 2024 (3:681-693), compared 8 open tools on an independent patent-image test set: DECIMER, ReactionDataExtractor, MolScribe, RxnScribe, SwinOCSR, OCMR, MolVec, OSRA. Tools showed different strengths per image modality and chemistry category, and overall performance was judged unsatisfactory. The authors trained a 4-class image classifier to route each image to the best tool. Per-tool numbers were not retrieved here. Ensembles similarly help: ChemExpert (DECIMER plus AtomLenz) is best on hand-drawn images, and VERDICT's 4-recognizer agreement gives about 0.99 precision at 52% coverage.
  - checker note: pubs.rsc.org was unreachable, so the Krasnov et al. specifics (8 tools, patent test set, 4-class routing classifier) could not be checked. They match my recollection of the abstract (Digital Discovery 2024, 3, 681). ChemExpert's best-on-hand-drawn result is verified (0.635/0.518). The VERDICT ensemble numbers are unverifiable (see F18).
  - sources: https://pubs.rsc.org/en/content/articlelanding/2024/dd/d3dd00228d ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01743 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22183
- [FIXED] **F38** (license) Key open datasets exist for training and evaluating a drug-discovery OCSR VLM, but several of the largest carry non-commercial licenses or no declared license.
  - details: MolParser-7M: 7.72M synthetic plus 91,166 real SFT plus WildMol 20k; CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0, commercial use by contact. MolGallery: about 10.3M pseudo-labeled real crops (193 GB); gated, CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0. GTR-CoT-1.3M: corrected USPTO plus PubChem with traversal CoT. MolScribe training data: USPTO-680K patent image/MOL pairs plus PubChem renders. MarkushGrapher-2-Datasets: USPTO-MOL-M 54,785 train plus 200 test, M2S 103, USPTO-Markush 74, IP5-M 878 test rows. MarkushGrapher-Datasets: 235k synthetic. MolRecBench-Wild: 5,024 test images with CARBON graphs, A/B/C subsets and hard-case labels; no license on the card. MolMole_Patent300: 300 full patent pages. Stereo-200k (MolSight). DECIMER hand-drawn: 5,088 images.
  - CORRECTION: The core claims hold: MolParser-7M and MolGallery are CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0; MolRecBench-Wild declares no license; MolMole_Patent300's card also declares none. Four corrections. (1) No public release of GTR-CoT-1.3M was found: no HF repo, no link in the paper, and github.com/opendatalab/GTR-CoT returns 404. Treat it as unreleased. (2) Some Markush data is permissively licensed: MarkushGrapher-Datasets (about 235k synthetic training rows plus M2S, USPTO-Markush and the 1k synthetic test) is CC-BY-4.0, and MarkushGrapher-2 weights are Apache-2.0. The MarkushGrapher-2-Datasets card shows no license tag. (3) Stereo-200k is on HF as Robert-zwr/Stereo-200k (Apache-2.0). (4) COMO's corrected 10-benchmark suite is on HF (Keylab/OCSR-Benchmarks, mixed licenses) with weights under CC-BY-NC-4.0.
  - checker note: Row counts verified: 7,720,468 / 91,166 / 403 / 10k+10k; MolGallery 10,327,534 rows; USPTO-MOL-M 54,785 train + 200 test; IP5-M 878 rows; MolRecBench-Wild 5,024 rows split A 1,987 / B 1,976 / C 1,061.
  - sources: https://huggingface.co/datasets/UniParser/MolParser-7M ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/UniParser/MolGallery ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/docling-project/MarkushGrapher-2-Datasets ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/opendatalab/MolRecBench-Wild ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/doxa-friend/MolMole_Patent300 ; https://github.com/hustvl/MolSight ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/docling-project/MarkushGrapher-Datasets ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/Robert-zwr/Stereo-200k ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/Keylab/OCSR-Benchmarks
- [ADDED] A pinned, corrected 10-benchmark OCSR suite and COMO weights are public on HF. This is a ready-made fixed evaluator for an autoresearch-style prepare.py.
  - details: Keylab/OCSR-Benchmarks contains CLEF 992, JPO 449, UOB 5,740, USPTO 5,719, USPTO-10K 9,999, Staker 50,000, ACS 331, WildMol-10K 9,889 (10,000 minus COMO's 111 removals), Indigo 5,719 and ChemDraw 5,719. It includes the alphaExtractor-corrected CLEF/JPO/UOB; licenses are mixed per source. Keylab/COMO holds the weights (CC-BY-NC-4.0). These are the exact versions behind COMO Table 1, so a scalar 'mean EM over pinned shards' can be reproduced. Note that WildMol scoring in this suite expands abbreviations, unlike MolParser's protocol.
  - sources: https://huggingface.co/datasets/Keylab/OCSR-Benchmarks ; https://huggingface.co/Keylab/COMO ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546
- [ADDED] MolParser-Mobile (about 10M params) and MolParser-Mobile-V2 ('E-SMILES 2.0') weights, MolDetv2 (plus a YOLO26 variant) and MolDet-Bench are publicly released.
  - details: At 1,520 molecules/s on one RTX 4090D these are practical as a pseudo-labeler, ensemble voter, or fast inner-loop scorer within a 5-minute experiment budget. They also give a strong small baseline that a fine-tuned VLM must beat. License is CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0. The V2 card advertises an updated E-SMILES 2.0 output format that the findings do not mention.
  - sources: https://huggingface.co/UniParser/MolParser-Mobile-V2 ; https://huggingface.co/papers/2609.05807 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807
- [ADDED] BioMiner/BioVista (Yan et al., bioRxiv 2025) is the main public benchmark for joint structure-plus-bioactivity mining from papers. That is the property half of the user's goal, which the findings largely skip.
  - details: BioVista covers protein-ligand bioactivity extraction from 500 papers (11,212 molecular instances). An OCSR split is also used by Uni-Parser and MolParser-Mobile. Both BioVista and BioVista-OCSR are on HF under MIT. It is the natural held-out evaluator for structure-activity pairing (IC50/Ki with target and units), which no OCSR benchmark covers.
  - sources: https://huggingface.co/datasets/jiaxianustc/BioVista ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/jiaxianustc/BioVista-OCSR ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15098
- [ADDED] OpenChemIE (Coley/Barzilay, arXiv 2404.01462, MIT) is the main open end-to-end baseline for linking structures to identifiers and text.
  - details: It integrates MolScribe, RxnScribe, MolDetect, MolCoref (molecule-label coreference, i.e. linking '1a' to its structure), ChemNER, ChemRxnExtractor and PDF table extraction. The repo also links an R-group-resolution evaluation dataset. It is directly relevant to identifier linking and SAR/substrate tables, and MolMole uses it as a baseline.
  - sources: https://github.com/CrystalEye42/OpenChemIE ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03777
- [ADDED] Born-digital PDFs allow exact, label-free ground truth: ChemScraper parses molecule diagrams from PDF vector graphics without OCR.
  - details: ChemScraper (arXiv 2311.12161) extracts characters, lines and polygons from born-digital PDFs and converts them into a visual graph, then a chemical graph (CDXML). Its authors propose it explicitly as a generator of annotated training data for raster recognizers. For journal articles and SI, which are mostly born-digital, this is a cheap data-engine source complementary to MolParser-style active learning.
  - sources: https://huggingface.co/papers/2311.12161 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12161
- [ADDED] Uni-Parser decouples table structure from cell content and parses molecules inside table cells. This is the relevant design for R-group/SAR tables that carry activity values.
  - details: Uni-Parser uses SLANet trained on about 1M tables (cleaned PubTabNet, SynthTabNet, plus in-house synthetic tables with molecule, chart and formula cells), along with table orientation recovery and cross-page table merging. It reinserts molecules embedded in table cells after OCSR. Structure-activity tables need exactly this kind of table-plus-molecule handling, which the findings do not cover.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15098
- [ADDED] Some Markush training and evaluation data is permissively licensed, unlike the MolParser/MolGallery family.
  - details: MarkushGrapher-Datasets is CC-BY-4.0 (about 211.6k train + 23.6k synthetic rows, plus M2S 103 and USPTO-Markush 74). MarkushGrapher-2 weights are Apache-2.0 and the GitHub repo MIT. MarkushGlyph/OCSRGlyph code is Apache-2.0. Stereo-200k (MolSight) is Apache-2.0. For a commercial drug-discovery user these are the main reusable sources; MolParser-7M, MolGallery and MolParser-Mobile are non-commercial.
  - sources: https://huggingface.co/datasets/docling-project/MarkushGrapher-Datasets ; https://huggingface.co/docling-project/MarkushGrapher-2 ; https://github.com/EdisonScientific/glyph ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/Robert-zwr/Stereo-200k
- [ADDED] DianShi-RxnDB (Sept 2026, the same Shanghai AI Lab group as MinerU.Chem/MolRecBench-Wild) shows how to evaluate field-level extraction at patent scale.
  - details: It is a fully automated USPTO+EPO 1976-2025 pipeline over text, images and schemes. It yields about 24M reaction instances, of which about 14.8M (61.7%) pass automated qualification. Micro-averaged field-level accuracy is 92.95% on 1,300 manually checked instances, with a matched comparison against Pistachio and an MCP service for agents. The sampled field-level-accuracy protocol is a usable template for scoring property extraction (value, unit, qualifier, assay, target), which needs its own metric beyond OCSR exact match.
  - sources: https://huggingface.co/papers/2609.06703 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06703
- [ADDED] PatCID (Morin et al., Nature Communications 15:6532, 2024; IBM) is an open dataset of chemical structures extracted from patents across offices, built by the MolGrapher team.
  - details: COMO cites it as a key real-patent resource. It offers large-scale coverage of patent images and structures beyond USPTO for training or for retrieval-based validation of patent extraction. The record links images to patent documents. Exact counts were not verified here because nature.com was unreachable.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546 ; https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-50779-y
- [ADDED] Reaction-diagram parsing work from the MinerU.Chem group supplies reusable pieces: MolYOLO detection (RxnCaption) and identifier visual prompting plus Re3-DAPO RL (RxnID, with the ScannedRxn benchmark released).
  - details: RxnCaption (arXiv 2511.02384) introduced MolYOLO, reported at P/R 0.98/0.98 on MolDet-33k-test. RxnID (arXiv 2603.15011) uses molecule identifiers such as '1a' as visual prompts and optimizes reaction-level metrics with verifiable-reward RL. Its RxnID model and the ScannedRxn dataset (200 scanned historical diagrams) are on HF. Both bear on identifier linking and on RL with task-level rewards for an extraction VLM.
  - sources: https://huggingface.co/papers/2603.15011 ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/songjhPKU/ScannedRxn ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525

### Implications (researcher's recommendations)
- Treat prepare.py as a frozen, versioned OCSR evaluation harness: pinned image sets with checksums, a fixed RDKit version, and one canonicalization path. The scalar metric must not depend on the output representation, just as val_bpb does not depend on vocab size. Convert every model output (SMILES, E-SMILES, CXSMILES, CARBON/graph, MOLfile) to a standardized molecule, then score. Recommended primary scalar: val_err = 1 − exact-match rate, where exact match = RDKit canonical SMILES (or InChIKey) equality after standard normalization: keep tetrahedral chirality, ignore cis/trans unless the label has it, map R-groups to [d*], standardize tautomers. Log graph-level EM and Tanimoto as secondary columns only.
- Build the pinned validation set from data that resembles drug-discovery documents, not classic patents alone. Include a fast slice of about 1.5-2K crops: stratified samples of WildMol-10K, MolRecBench-Wild (2026-08-19 release, subsets A/B/C), corrected CLEF/JPO/UOB, ACS-331, and a few hundred in-house medchem SI and patent crops with stereo and abbreviations. Add a Markush slice (IP5-M/M2S/USPTO-M scored with the official MarkushGrapher-2 scorer). The fast slice drives keep/discard; run the full suites (all of WildMol, MolRecBench-Wild, IP5-M, USPTO-10K-L, Staker) nightly to catch overfitting to the slice.
- Combine slices into one number with fixed weights (e.g. 0.5 real-literature crops + 0.2 hard subset C/stereo + 0.2 Markush + 0.1 classic patents). Also log per-slice scores in results.tsv. Add a guardrail: a change that wins on the scalar but drops any slice by more than X pts is auto-discarded. This prevents the agent from trading Markush or stereo ability for easy-image gains.
- Adapt the 5-minute budget to VLM fine-tuning. Use a small base (Qwen3-VL-2B/4B or Qwen3.5-2B; MarkushGlyph shows 2B+LoRA is enough for Markush SOTA). Start each experiment from a pinned SFT checkpoint. Give each run a fixed wall-clock budget (e.g. 20-30 min of training on one H100) plus a separately capped evaluation: fixed max_new_tokens, greedy decoding, vLLM batch. That allows about 2-3 experiments/hour and about 30-40 overnight. Make long-run checks (full SFT from base) a separate, rarer outer loop.
- Give the agent the levers the literature shows matter, all inside train.py: (a) real/synthetic mixture ratio, the strongest lever (ACS 0.15→0.46); (b) augmentation curriculum from weak to strong (WildMol 40.1→76.9); (c) output representation (plain SMILES vs E-SMILES vs graph-traversal CoT vs CXSMILES); (d) vision-tower LoRA on/off and per-branch learning rates (effect depends on the base model; split LR stabilized MolSeek); (e) image resolution and aspect-ratio padding; (f) chiral, abbreviation and large-molecule oversampling; (g) an optional reward-based stage: MRT/GRPO/DAPO with a binary structure-identity reward (canonical-molecule equality or Tanimoto=1) plus validity, with difficulty-filtered prompts; (h) test-time majority vote (MV@k gave +1-4 pts for MarkushGlyph).
- Keep data production (the 'data engine') outside the fast loop, as a human-gated process that feeds new frozen data versions. Use ensemble disagreement for active learning: sample crops with mean pairwise Tanimoto 0.6-0.9, as in MolParser. Accept pseudo-labels only on multi-recognizer agreement by molecular identity: 3-of-4 agreement gave 0.995 precision in VERDICT, and MolGallery used 3-model unanimity. Ensure labels are faithful to the image (keep abbreviations as superatoms; remap '*' to drawn labels). The agent should not edit data versions; bump them deliberately, like program.md revisions.
- Add leakage and dedup checks to prepare.py. Classic eval sets (USPTO, CLEF, USPTO-10K, MolRec-USPTO) come from the same USPTO image pool as training sets (USPTO-680K, USPTO-MOL-M, GTR-CoT-USPTO-351K). Remove eval molecules from training by InChIKey and by image p-hash, and reject runs whose training manifest intersects the eval manifest.
- Harden the scorer against metric gaming before letting an autonomous agent optimize it. Count unparseable or invalid outputs as wrong. Forbid emitting '*' for drawn abbreviations unless the label has a wildcard: score abbreviations both expanded and as superatoms and require the expanded match. Use a substance filter like VERDICT's against wildcard-only fragments. Check stereo explicitly so Tanimoto=1 cannot hide stereoisomer errors.
- Prefer an output that can be grounded back to the image: graph with coordinates or CARBON-like JSON, exported to SMILES/MOLfile/CXSMILES in post-processing outside train.py. It speeds expert review about 2x (MolScribe: 20 s vs 39 s per molecule), enables atom-level confidence, and keeps Markush, polymer and non-standard bonds representable for SAR. If the agent explores pure-SMILES captioning, require it to beat the graph baseline on the stereo and abbreviation slices.
- Establish strong pinned baselines in the first rows of results.tsv so improvements are meaningful. Open specialists: MolScribe, MolNexTR, MarkushGrapher-2, OCSRGlyph/MarkushGlyph, MolSight. A frontier API model (zero-shot ~65-75% on real crops). The off-the-shelf base VLM (near 0%). Note that MolParser 1.5 and MinerU.Chem's GTR-VL-1.4.13 may only be reachable as services or with non-commercial terms.
- Check licenses before building the training corpus for a commercial drug-discovery deployment. MolParser-7M and MolGallery are CC-BY-NC-SA, and MolRecBench-Wild declares no license. Prefer Apache/MIT assets (MarkushGrapher-2 data and code, Glyph, MolSight, MolScribe/USPTO-680K, PubChem renders) plus in-house annotations for anything shipped.
- Scope the recognizer loop to single crops. Freeze detection (MolDetv2/YOLO, about 0.99 mAP50), identifier linking and table/property extraction as separate components with their own pinned metrics, possibly their own later autoresearch loops. Add an end-to-end page-level F1 (detection IoU≥0.5 AND exact structure match, as in MolMole) as a nightly report, not the inner-loop objective.
- Write the program.md simplicity criterion in OCSR terms. Reject complexity such as extra post-processing heuristics, special-case regexes or ensembles that buys less than about 0.3 pts on the scalar. Accept deletions that keep the score. Require that any RL/MRT stage beats an equal-compute SFT control on the same data: COMO's ablation showed extra data under MLE can hurt while MRT helps, and MolSeek's GSPO hurt EM.

### Open questions
- Can a general VLM fine-tuned on public plus in-house data reach MinerU.Chem-level real-literature accuracy (93% SMILES / 80% graph on revised MolRecBench-Wild)? How much of GTR-VL-1.4.13's gain comes from its proprietary human-annotated and difficulty-targeted synthetic data rather than architecture or representation?
- Which output representation is best for downstream SAR and property analysis? Graph-with-coordinates (CARBON-like), E-SMILES and CXSMILES each cover different Markush, polymer and non-standard-bond cases, and none supports automatic enumeration from Markush scaffolds plus R-group tables.
- Does RL (GRPO/DAPO/MRT) reliably add exact-match accuracy on real crops for large VLMs, or mainly on smaller specialist decoders? Evidence conflicts: COMO and ChemVLR positive, MolSeek-OCR GSPO negative, MolSight's RL variant not better than its 'extra' variant.
- What is the realistic accuracy ceiling given label noise and representational ambiguity in public benchmarks? Should the loop's primary metric be canonical-SMILES EM, InChIKey EM, or graph EM?
- How large and which in-house real-crop annotation budget gives the best return for drug-discovery literature and SI, which differ in style from USPTO patents? The 2026 'Real Data' study suggests about 10-50% real fraction matters, but absolute counts for medchem documents are unknown.
- Are MinerU.Chem (GTR-VL-1.4.13), MolParser 1.5 and MolMole ViMore available as weights for distillation or pseudo-labeling, and under what license?
- 'AlphaOCSR' named in the task brief could not be found in any searched source. It may not exist under that name.
- The web-search budget ran out partway through. Not verified from primary tables: MolNexTR's own per-benchmark results (arXiv 2403.03691), DECIMER.ai's Nature Communications 2023 benchmark numbers, per-tool numbers in Krasnov et al. 2024, BioMiner/BioVista bioactivity-extraction results, and ChemDFM-X standalone OCSR numbers. Exact GTR-CoT Table 1 values and the 'Real Data' and VERDICT figures come from search-engine summaries and should be checked before citing.

## Topic: doc-chem-ie

### Summary
Document-level chemical information extraction (CIE) has split into two layers by 2026. The first is general PDF parsing. Most leading parsers (MinerU2.5/Pro, PaddleOCR-VL, dots.ocr, olmOCR, Marker, MonkeyOCR, SmolDocling, Docling core, Mistral OCR) still do not emit SMILES; on LogicsParsingBench most score the worst possible chemistry edit distance of 1.0. A few, mainly GOT-OCR2, DeepSeek-OCR, Logics-Parsing and Mathpix, emit SMILES for clean single molecules, but with poor accuracy on real images. The second layer is chemistry-specific systems. These are almost all modular pipelines that bolt specialist modules onto a general parser: a group-aware layout or molecule detector, an OCSR model (MolParser/E-SMILES, MolScribe, GTR-VL/CARBON, MolGrapher), a molecule-to-identifier linker, a table parser, R-group/Markush expansion and an LLM or MLLM reasoner. Examples are Uni-Parser, MinerU.Chem, MolMole, BioMiner, Doc2SAR, BioChemInsight, ChemEagle, OpenChemIE and MERMaid. On real literature, specialist OCSR models clearly beat frontier general VLMs. On MolRecBench-Wild (revised), MinerU.Chem reaches 93.0% SMILES exact match against 74.9% for the best frontier VLM. On BioVista, MolParser-Mobile scores 0.80 against 0.35 for Qwen3.5-397B and 0.12 for Gemma4-31B. Reaction diagram parsing shows the same pattern: VLMs fail when asked to predict boxes, but do well when a detector supplies indexed boxes or identifiers as visual prompts (Gemini-2.5-Pro soft F1 24.3 rises to 76.1). The end-to-end document task, linking structure to activity to target, remains far from solved. BioMiner's best triplet F1 on BioVista is about 0.32-0.33, even though value recall is about 0.85 and structure recall about 0.58. Errors compound across detection, OCSR, coreference, Markush enumeration and target linking. Doc2SAR reaches 80.8% table recall against 29.3% for end-to-end GPT-4o. Patents add their own problems: Markush claims versus exemplified compounds, compounds given as example numbers or IUPAC names, and binned or qualified activity values. They also offer unusual free supervision: USPTO complex-work-unit MOL/CDX files (MolScribe used 680K image-MOL pairs) and BindingDB's curated patent records (8,457 patents and about 1.29M measurements by Dec 2025). Recent training recipes converge on a data-centric approach: multi-model consensus pseudo-labels at the 10M scale, active learning, human SFT sets, then RL or preference training on molecule-level or record-level verifiable rewards (Re3-DAPO, COMO-MRT, olmOCR-2 unit tests, MolParser-Mobile DPO). MinerU2.5-Pro showed +2.71 points from data engineering alone with the architecture frozen. For an autoresearch-style loop, the strongest lessons are four. Pin a human-verified, time-split, document-level evaluation set. Use a single order-invariant, chemistry-canonicalised record-level F1. Let the agent edit the data mixture, prompting and visual-prompting strategy, and the reward, not only the model. Treat the specialist tools plus a VLM reader as the baseline the fine-tuned VLM must beat.

### Findings
- [OK] **F1** (number) On LogicsParsingBench, almost all mainstream document parsers produce no usable chemical structures; the best SMILES edit distance is only about 0.52-0.55.
  - details: Logics-Parsing technical report (Alibaba, arXiv 2509.19760), Table 1 'Chemistry Edit' column: normalized edit distance (NED) between predicted and ground-truth SMILES strings over all chemistry elements on the 1,078-page LogicsParsingBench (lower is better; 1.0 means nothing usable was produced). Scores: doc2x 1.0, TextIn 1.0, PP-StructureV3 1.0, MinerU2 1.0, Marker 1.0, pix2text 1.0, dots.ocr 1.0, MonkeyOCR 1.0, OCRFlux 1.0, olmOCR 1.0, SmolDocling 1.0, Dolphin 0.984, GOT-OCR 0.969, GPT-5 0.88, Qwen2-VL-72B 0.792, doubao-1.6 0.601, Qwen2.5-VL-72B 0.597, Mathpix 0.552, Gemini 2.5 Pro 0.535, Logics-Parsing 0.519. Logics-Parsing is Qwen2.5-VL-7B with SFT on >300K pages, including ChEBI-20-MM molecule images, followed by layout-centric GRPO on about 8K hard pages. Takeaway: SMILES output is natively present only in a few end-to-end VLM parsers, and even the best is weak.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.19760 ; https://huggingface.co/papers/2509.19760 ; hf://papers/2509.19760/paper.md
- [FIXED] **F2** (existence) GOT-OCR2.0 and DeepSeek-OCR natively emit SMILES for molecule images through 'OCR-2.0' prompts, trained purely on RDKit-rendered synthetic data, but publish no OCSR accuracy figures and fail on Markush structures.
  - details: GOT-OCR2.0 (580M parameters, arXiv 2409.01704) built about 1M molecular-formula image-text pairs from ChEMBL_25 (2M SMILES) using Mathpix-markdown-it and RDKit; output format is selectable by prompt (markdown/tikz/smiles/kern). DeepSeek-OCR (arXiv 2510.18234) built 5M image-text pairs from PubChem SMILES rendered with RDKit; its 'deep parsing' mode converts chemical formulas in documents to SMILES (shown qualitatively in Fig. 8 only). Independent numbers: LogicsParsingBench chemistry NED for GOT-OCR is 0.969. The MarkushGrapher repo reports DeepSeek-OCR at 0% CXSMILES accuracy on M2S and USPTO-M, 1.9% on WildMol-M and 0.0% on IP5-M. Lesson: purely synthetic render data does not transfer to real literature or patent depictions.
  - CORRECTION: GOT-OCR2.0 has 580M parameters. It built ~1M molecular-formula image-text pairs from ChEMBL_25 (2M SMILES) with Mathpix-markdown-it and RDKit, and its output format (markdown/tikz/smiles/kern) is chosen by prompt. DeepSeek-OCR rendered 5M PubChem SMILES with RDKit; its deep-parsing SMILES output is shown only qualitatively in Fig. 8. Neither paper reports OCSR accuracy. Markush failure is documented only for DeepSeek-OCR: 0 / 0 / 1.9 / 0.0 CXSMILES accuracy on M2S / USPTO-M / WildMol-M / IP5-M in the MarkushGrapher README. No Markush evaluation of GOT-OCR2.0 was found; its only independent chemistry figure is NED 0.969 on LogicsParsingBench.
  - checker note: All numbers check out. The overreach is the claim that both models fail on Markush. 'Synthetic renders do not transfer' is the analyst's inference, not something either paper shows.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01704 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18234 ; https://github.com/DS4SD/MarkushGrapher
- [OK] **F3** (existence) MinerU (core 2.x/2.5) and PaddleOCR-VL have no chemistry output. Chemistry appears only as add-ons: the MinerU.Chem online mode, and Docling, whose figure classifier tags molecule images that an external MolGrapher script then converts to SMILES.
  - details: The MinerU GitHub README has no mention of chemistry, SMILES or OCSR. MinerU.Chem (arXiv 2608.03525, Aug 2026) is a 'Chemistry Paper' option on mineru.net layered on top of MinerU's Markdown and layout output. A grep of the PaddleOCR-VL paper (arXiv 2510.14528) finds no chemistry mentions. Docling's DocumentFigureClassifier (EfficientNet-B0, 16 classes) includes 'chemistry_molecular_structure' and 'chemistry_markush_structure'. The DS4SD/MolGrapher repo provides docling_convert_and_enrich.sh, which adds SMILES annotations to the Docling document. A search found no documented SMILES output from Mistral OCR. MinerU2.5-Pro (arXiv 2604.04771) improved OmniDocBench v1.6 from 92.98 to 95.69 through data engineering alone (training data grew from under 10M to 65.5M samples, plus cross-model consistency labels, judge-and-refine annotation and GRPO), with the 1.2B architecture fixed.
  - sources: https://github.com/opendatalab/MinerU ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525 ; https://huggingface.co/ds4sd/DocumentFigureClassifier ; https://github.com/DS4SD/MolGrapher ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04771 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14528
- [OK] **F4** (number) MinerU.Chem (Shanghai AI Lab, 2026) adds five chemistry modules to MinerU and reaches 93.02% SMILES exact match on the revised MolRecBench-Wild, against 74.87% for the best frontier VLM. It does not yet expand Markush or R-group tables.
  - details: Modules: chemistry relevance filter, MolYOLO molecule detection (P/R@IoU0.5 = 0.98/0.98 on MolDet-33k-test, against MolDetect 0.84/0.77), molecule-identifier extraction (Mid-Mapper), OCSR with GTR-VL-1.4.13 predicting a native CARBON graph that is exported to MolFile/SMILES, and reaction parsing (RxnCaption-based). Outputs are a Molecule Summary List and a Reaction Summary List with page, bbox and identifier provenance. Revised MolRecBench-Wild: SMILES exact match on N=2,392 SMILES-evaluable images; graph accuracy (exact topology plus atom and bond attributes) on N=5,024. Full SMILES/graph scores: MinerU.Chem 93.02/79.66; GPT-5.6-Sol 74.87/32.19; Gemini-3.5-flash-thinking 66.85/36.41; Claude-opus-4-8 65.47/14.05; MolNexTR 62.50; MolScribe 62.29; Mathpix 47.32; DECIMER v2.2 41.43; InternVL3.5 39.80/3.05; ChemVLM 8.03. On the hardest subset C (chemical-semantic difficulty labels), MinerU.Chem drops to 70.13 SMILES and 55.42 graph. The paper states that automated Markush/R-group expansion is future work.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525 ; https://huggingface.co/papers/2608.03525 ; hf://papers/2608.03525/paper.md
- [FIXED] **F5** (number) MolRecBench-Wild shows that OCSR on real journal images is much harder than classic patent benchmarks, and that headline numbers move a lot between benchmark versions.
  - details: arXiv 2605.05832 (Aug 2026): 5,029 structures from about 820 CC-BY papers in 7 chemistry journals. The MOSAIC difficulty taxonomy has 18 visual and 19 chemical-semantic labels; 93.29% of samples carry at least one label and 42% carry labels in both dimensions. Ground truth is in CARBON (supports non-standard bonds, oxidation states, radicals, repeating units and image coordinates) plus SMILES where expressible. Annotation took 47 chemistry annotators and 152 person-days. v1 SMILES accuracy: MolScribe 41.05, MolNexTR 40.90, GTR-Mol-VLM 40.43, Gemini 2.5 Pro 30.06, Mathpix 27.88, InternVL3.5 25.60, DECIMER 22.84, GPT-5 19.68, GPT-4o 7.94. Full-graph accuracy is at most 13.04 (Gemini 2.5 Pro). For comparison, MolGrapher and MolParser exceed 90% on USPTO/UOB. MolScribe's score went from 41.05 (v1) to 62.29 on the revised version used by MinerU.Chem, which changed the SMILES-evaluable subset. Benchmark versioning therefore matters a great deal.
  - CORRECTION: MolRecBench-Wild is arXiv 2605.05832 (May 2026, CVPR 2026), not Aug 2026. The v1 paper has 5,029 structures from ~820 CC-BY-4.0 papers in 7 journals. The authoritative Hugging Face release (2026-08-19) has 5,024 images from 818 articles with A/B/C subsets, and its card declares no license. The v1 numbers are correct: MolScribe 41.05, MolNexTR 40.90, GTR-Mol-VLM 40.43, Gemini 2.5 Pro 30.06, Mathpix 27.88, InternVL3.5 25.60, DECIMER 22.84, GPT-5 19.68, GPT-4o 7.94. The maximum full-Graph accuracy of 13.04 (Gemini 2.5 Pro) is among VLMs only; graph-expert models were not scored on the full Graph track. 93.29% of samples carry at least one label, 42% carry labels in both dimensions, 47 annotators spent 152 person-days, and every sample went through at least 2 QC rounds.
  - checker note: The point about benchmark versioning holds. The revised release restricts SMILES scoring to 2,392 evaluable images, and on it MolScribe scores 62.29 against 41.05 on v1.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05832 ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/opendatalab/MolRecBench-Wild ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525
- [OK] **F6** (method) Uni-Parser (DP Technology) is an industrial modular multi-expert parser for papers and patents. Its layout detector groups each molecule with its identifier and Markush description, it uses MolParser 1.5 for OCSR, and it reports 20 pages/s on 8x RTX 4090D.
  - details: arXiv 2512.15098 (Dec 2025, revised 2026; CC BY-NC-SA). The group-based layout model (Uni-Parser-LD, modified DETR, 1024x768 input) was trained on 500k pages, 220k of them human-annotated, across 85 languages. It pairs figure-caption, table-title/footnote, molecule-identifier/Markush description and equation-ID. Tables are recognised by SLANet (trained on 1M tables), with molecules and other content re-inserted into cells, and tables and reaction schemes are merged across pages. MolParser 1.5 adds 10M real images pseudo-labeled by cross-validation over several MolParser models and a fine-tuned MolScribe. Throughput: 240 L40 GPUs parsed more than 16M documents in under 6 days. The training corpus covers 170M literature pages and 140M patent pages; an active-learning and self-training flywheel cut annotation volume by 95%. Reported failures: (1) synthetic layout pretraining such as DocSynth300K was counterproductive once enough real data existed; (2) graph or atom-bond OCSR needed more than 20x the annotation effort of end-to-end E-SMILES and had a lower ceiling. The comparison of PDF tools that claim OCSR used only 141 simple molecules and is labelled qualitative; its numbers were not retrievable.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15098 ; https://huggingface.co/papers/2512.15098 ; hf://papers/2512.15098/paper.md
- [OK] **F7** (number) MolParser-Mobile (DP Technology, Sep 2026) is a 9.98M-parameter OCSR model running at 1,520 molecules/s that beats general VLMs by a wide margin. It was trained on 10M consensus pseudo-labels, followed by SFT, on-policy distillation and DPO.
  - details: arXiv 2609.05807. BioVista OCSR accuracy (overall/chiral/Markush/full): MolParser-Mobile 0.801/0.653/0.805/0.797; MolParser 1.0 0.703/0.352/0.733/0.669; MolMiner 0.507; MolScribe 0.455; MolNexTR 0.401; DECIMER 0.298. Uni-Parser Bench overall: MolParser-Mobile 0.823, MolParser 0.800, MolScribe 0.417. General VLMs on UniParserBench/BioVista: Gemma4-31B 0.073/0.118; Qwen3.5-397B 0.272/0.347. MolGallery is about 10M real-document crops whose labels were kept only when 3 OCSR models (2 MolParser variants and MolScribe) gave identical E-SMILES. Recipe: pretraining on 18M pairs (7.72M synthetic MolParser-7M plus 10.3M MolGallery), SFT on 91.2k human labels plus a 200k replay buffer, on-policy distillation from MolParser 1.5, then DPO on 500 hand-corrected preference pairs targeting Markush and chirality errors. MolDetv2-Doc-n reaches mAP50 0.9936 on MolDet-Bench-Doc (447 pages, 2,178 molecules) in 3.1 ms on a T4.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05807 ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/UniParser/MolGallery ; https://huggingface.co/UniParser/MolDetv2
- [OK] **F8** (number) MolMole (LG AI Research) processes whole pages without a separate layout parser and reports page-level molecule detection+OCSR F1 of 0.891 on patents and 0.868 on papers. Elsevier adopted it for Reaxys curation in 2026.
  - details: arXiv 2505.03777. Modules: ViDetect (DINO/DETR), ViReact (RxnScribe-style, page-level) and ViMore (detection-based atom/bond OCSR that produces layout-preserving MOLfiles, handles wavy bonds and polymer brackets, and gives low/medium/high confidence). The benchmark has 550 pages (300 patent, 250 article) with 3,897 molecules and 1,022 reactions. Combined metric: a true positive requires IoU>=0.5 and an exact SMILES match; P/R/F1 = 0.895/0.887/0.891 on patents and 0.867/0.868/0.868 on articles. Detection AP 0.914 (patents) and 0.928 (articles). OCSR with ground-truth boxes: 90% and 88% SMILES match. Reaction parsing F1 soft/hard: 0.980/0.925 on patents and 0.970/0.846 on articles, against RxnScribe without layout parser at 0.822/0.490 on patents. RxnScribe without a layout parser beat RxnScribe with one, so cropping errors propagate. Public OCSR InChI accuracy: USPTO 93.8, CLEF 85.3, JPO 81.5. Elsevier's press release (Sep 2026) says it is used in Reaxys extraction but gives no accuracy figures.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03777 ; https://www.elsevier.com/about/press-releases/elsevier-and-lg-ai-research-unlock-more-chemistry-hidden-in-scientific-images ; https://chemistryworld.com/news/elsevier-teams-up-with-ai-firm-to-extract-chemical-structure-data-from-papers/4024245.article ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/doxa-friend/MolMole_Patent300
- [OK] **F9** (number) BioMiner/BioVista, the largest protein-ligand bioactivity extraction benchmark, shows that end-to-end structure-activity-target extraction remains poor (triplet F1 about 0.32). The bottleneck is joining structures to values, not reading the values.
  - details: BioVista: 500 PDBbind-v2020 publications, 16,457 bioactivity labels and 8,735 structures. Tasks: bioactivity triplet extraction (P/R/F1); structure-bioactivity annotation (Recall@N); molecule detection (AP, 11,212 boxes); OCSR (8,861 depictions); full-structure coreference (962 images, 5,105 pairs); Markush enumeration (355 images, 3,513 scaffold-R-group-coreference pairs). BioMiner uses MinerU parsing, MolDetV2, MolGlyph OCSR, RDKit/OPSIN and a 32B BioMiner-Instruct MLLM, at about 20 s per paper on 4 A800s. The repo changelog reports triplet F1 rising from 0.22 (Apr 2025) to 0.32 (Dec 2025); the abstract says 0.33 for complex triplets. Example end-to-end output: bioactivity recall 0.654 and precision 0.614; recall without protein 0.778; structure recall 0.580 and precision 0.615; joint structure+bioactivity recall 0.328 and precision 0.319; value recall 0.850. OCSR on BioVista: MolGlyph 0.764 overall (Markush 0.770), MolParser 0.703, MolMiner 0.507, MolScribe 0.455. Uses: 82,262 EJMC records gave +3.9% on downstream models; a human-in-the-loop NLRP3 set of about 1.6K records; PoseBusters annotation 5.59x faster.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21508 ; https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2025.04.22.648951 ; https://github.com/jiaxianyan/BioMiner
- [UNVERIFIED] **F10** (number) Doc2SAR combines specialist tools with SFT-tuned Qwen2.5-VL modules and reaches 80.78% table recall on DocSAR-200, against 29.30% for end-to-end GPT-4o.
  - details: arXiv 2506.21625 (DP Technology, Jun/Oct 2025). DocSAR-200 has 200 documents (98 patents, 102 articles) with more than 3,300 SMILES and 2,600 tables. Pipeline: layout detection, OCSR (MolParser), a fine-tuned Qwen2.5-VL that resolves molecule IDs such as 'Example 212' from surrounding text (coreference), a fine-tuned MLLM that converts table images to HTML while preserving merged cells and headers, and an assembly step. SFT set: 9,790 samples, pre-labelled by Qwen2.5-VL-7B and checked by 2 humans. The metric is 'Table Recall' (recovery of SAR table rows). Ablation numbers were not retrieved. An unverified 2026 'PatSAR' report claims 81.86% table recall on a patent subset against 74.09% for Doc2SAR; it could not be confirmed.
  - checker note: Neither arXiv nor Hugging Face Papers was reachable for this paper. The Doc2SAR repo README shows '0.8078 # Average Table Recall' only as example output, which is consistent with 80.78%. The repo does not give GPT-4o's 29.30%, the DocSAR-200 composition (98/102, >3,300 SMILES, 2,600 tables), the pipeline components, or the 9,790 SFT samples. PatSAR stays unverified.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21625 ; https://github.com/Jesse-zjx/Doc2SAR
- [UNVERIFIED] **F11** (number) BioChemInsight is an open patent pipeline (DECIMER segmentation, OCSR, VLM identifier linking, OCR+LLM bioactivity extraction) that reports more than 90% task accuracy on patents. Accuracy on non-tabular patents is much lower.
  - details: arXiv 2504.10525. v1: DECIMER Segmentation + MolVec + Qwen2.5-VL-32B + PaddleOCR + Gemini-2.0-flash, tested on 25 patents and 17 articles.
