Tracked as separate guardrails (invalid-SMILES rate, multi-fragment rate, valence-error rate), these expose degenerate strategies such as emitting placeholders that the main metric would otherwise absorb.
  - sources: https://github.com/jiaxianyan/BioMiner/blob/main/BioMiner/runner/metric_fn.py ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546 ; https://github.com/thomas0809/MolScribe/blob/main/molscribe/evaluate.py ; https://github.com/chembl/ChEMBL_Structure_Pipeline
- [UNVERIFIED] **F37** (lesson) Evaluating only on an independent, separately sourced patent image set shows that results on the familiar benchmarks overstate real-world accuracy. Krasnov et al. compared 8 open OCSR tools on their own patent images and judged them overall 'unsatisfactory'.
  - details: Krasnov et al., Digital Discovery 2024, 3, 681–693 (DOI 10.1039/D3DD00228D), evaluated DECIMER, ReactionDataExtractor, MolScribe, RxnScribe, SwinOCSR, OCMR, MolVec and OSRA on an independent patent set spanning inorganic complexes to peptides. They also built an image classifier that routes each image to the best tool and released the data openly. This supports holding out a privately curated, out-of-source eval set rather than reusing USPTO-derived public sets.
  - checker note: pubs.rsc.org was blocked from this environment. The paper (Krasnov et al., Digital Discovery 2024, comparing eight OCSR tools on independent patent images and adding a tool-selection classifier) matches my background knowledge. The exact 'unsatisfactory' wording, the page numbers and the data-release details were not checked.
  - sources: https://pubs.rsc.org/en/content/articlehtml/2024/dd/d3dd00228d
- [ADDED] Doc2SAR's value matcher ignores units and inequality direction, so its SAR 'Table Recall' cannot be trusted for activity values.
  - details: In utils/metrics.py, fuzzy_compare_value calls fuzzy_normalize_value, which extracts only the number and strips '<' and '>'. Running the scorer's code: '10 nM' vs '10 µM' gives True, '<10 nM' vs '>10 µM' gives True, '15 nM' vs '1' gives True (prefix match), and '1.23 nM' vs '1.25 nM' gives True (rounded to one decimal). A harness reusing this scorer would reward 1000x unit errors and flipped qualifiers. The value comparator must be rewritten with unit-aware, qualifier-aware, relative-tolerance rules before the scorer is used as a keep/discard metric.
  - sources: https://github.com/Jesse-zjx/Doc2SAR/blob/main/utils/metrics.py
- [ADDED] BioMiner's scorer drops invalid predicted rows before computing precision, so an agent can hide errors by emitting malformed rows.
  - details: load_labels_and_results applies filter_invalid_output_bioactivity (values must convert to positive nM) and filter_invalid_output_structure (RDKit-valid, no '*', at least 5 atoms, one fragment) to the extracted data only, with the comment 'filter invalid items for better precision result'. Rows with pIC50, % inhibition, unconvertible units or Markush/wildcard SMILES are removed from the precision denominator. A fixed harness should count every emitted row in precision and report invalid rows as a separate guardrail.
  - sources: https://github.com/jiaxianyan/BioMiner/blob/main/BioMiner/runner/metric_fn.py
- [ADDED] COMO shows that a sequence-level reward that includes an explicit exact-match term can raise strict exact match, which qualifies the RL lesson in F10.
  - details: COMO's MRT reward is 0.1·valid + 0.5·Sim·valid + 0.4·exact. It improved exact match over its MolScribe backbone on all 10 benchmarks (+12.2 JPO, +14.5 ACS, +10.7 WildMol-10K). In an ablation, adding the same 83K extra images under pure MLE degraded 5 of 10 benchmarks, so the gains come from the training objective and not from the data. MRT also transferred to SwinOCSR. For an autoresearch-style search, exact-match-weighted MRT/GRPO is a promising direction for train.py. MolSeek-OCR's GSPO, which put more weight on graph consistency, lowered exact match.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546
- [ADDED] Label-corrected OCSR benchmarks and model weights are public on HF and could serve as the pinned, read-only eval set in the prepare.py position.
  - details: Keylab/OCSR-Benchmarks has 10 configs, about 94.6K rows with image and SMILES: corrected CLEF (992), JPO (449), corrected UOB, USPTO (5,719), USPTO-10K (9,999), Staker (50K), ACS (331), Indigo, ChemDraw, and the expanded WildMol-10K (about 9.9K). The license is listed as 'other'. Keylab/COMO model weights are also on HF. Row counts differ slightly from the paper (JPO 449 vs 450; USPTO-10K 9,999 vs 10,000), so the dataset revision should be pinned.
  - sources: https://huggingface.co/datasets/Keylab/OCSR-Benchmarks ; https://huggingface.co/Keylab/COMO ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23546
- [ADDED] BioMiner (Dec 2025 upgrade) released open-weight components and a validation/test split of BioVista that maps directly onto autoresearch's val/test separation.
  - details: The releases are MolGlyph (OCSR, 'SOTA on BioVista': Markush 0.770, All 0.764 vs MolParser 0.733/0.703 and MolScribe 0.156/0.455) and BioMiner-Instruct (a 32B MLLM; triplet F1 0.323 vs Gemini-2.0-flash 0.272 and Qwen3-VL-235B 0.230). MolDetV2 is also used. BioVista was split 1:9 into validation and test. Only PDB IDs are distributed (no PDFs) because of copyright, so users must fetch the documents themselves. Its OCSR component has 8,861 depictions with Full, Chiral and Markush strata.
  - sources: https://github.com/jiaxianyan/BioMiner ; https://huggingface.co/jiaxianustc/MolGlyph ; https://huggingface.co/jiaxianustc/BioMiner-Instruct
- [ADDED] Directly fine-tuning all parameters of a document-OCR VLM on SMILES failed. A staged LoRA-then-selective-full-FT recipe was needed, which constrains the train.py search space for a VLM.
  - details: MolSeek-OCR reports 'Direct full-parameter SFT failed' on DeepSeek-OCR-2. Training used LoRA (rank 64; 192k or 96k samples, reported inconsistently) followed by full FT on 800k samples, with the visual tokenizer and embeddings frozen and split learning rates (5e-6 visual, 2e-5 language). It still reached only CLEF 63.3 / USPTO 61.0 / ACS 29.9, against MolScribe's 88.9 / 92.6 / 71.9. An autoresearch agent working on a VLM needs a time budget and guardrails that keep it from spending the night on unstable full-FT configurations. Image-to-graph heads, or graph-traversal CoT as in GTR-CoT, are also candidate directions.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03476 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07553
- [ADDED] MinerU.Chem (Aug 2026) uses CARBON, a layout-preserving molecular representation, instead of plain SMILES as the VLM output target, and reports 93.02% on MolRecBench-Wild.
  - details: CARBON (Complex Atomic Representation and Bonding Object Notation) keeps the image's visual layout and complex semantics (abbreviations, Markush) and exports MolFile and SMILES. It is part of a pipeline with relevance filtering, molecule detection, identifier (compound-ID) extraction, OCSR and reaction parsing. Its 93.02% vs GPT-5.6-Sol 74.87% is measured only on the SMILES-evaluable subset (N=2,392). For a harness, the choice of output representation (SMILES, CXSMILES, E-SMILES or CARBON) decides which structures can be scored at all. Unevaluable structures need to be reported, not silently dropped.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03525
- [ADDED] PostTrainBench enforces its no-test-training and no-model-substitution rules with an LLM judge after the fact. An autoresearch harness for VLM fine-tuning needs mechanical enforcement in addition.
  - details: In PostTrainBench, flagged runs receive the base-model score, but detection depends on an LLM judge reading traces. F35 shows such judges can be fooled, and BenchJack calls hack detectors 'gullible and unreliable'. For the drug-VLM harness, the stronger options are: test images and labels physically unavailable to the agent's process; checkpoint hash and provenance checks so a downloaded model cannot be substituted; scoring in a separate sandbox from model outputs only; and network or API-key isolation.
  - sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.08640 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12673
- [ADDED] MolMole released part of its page-level benchmark: a 300-page patent subset on HF that supports joint detection-and-recognition scoring.
  - details: doxa-friend/MolMole_Patent300 (300 rows, image folder) is linked from the MolMole paper. That paper's full test set has 550 pages, 3,897 molecules and 1,022 reactions; the article subset does not appear to be public. It is a ready-made source for page-level 'record counts only if box and SMILES are both right' evaluation, alongside BioVista and DocSAR-200.
  - sources: https://huggingface.co/datasets/doxa-friend/MolMole_Patent300 ; https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03777
- [ADDED] Verification-coverage caveat: several items could not be checked because of the environment.
  - details: arxiv.org, biorxiv.org, metr.org, rsc.org, bindingdb.org and PMC were blocked by the egress proxy, and the session's WebSearch budget was used up. Papers were read through the Hugging Face Papers mirror of arXiv where indexed, and code through raw GitHub. Items marked unverifiable (F18, F19, F20, F25, F28, F33, F34, F37) should be re-checked before they are relied on. The non-indexed 2026 preprints 2607.28532, 2608.09100 and 2608.30724 most of all.
  - sources: https://huggingface.co/papers

### Implications (researcher's recommendations)
- Copy autoresearch's 3-file split, but enforce prepare.py with real isolation rather than instructions. train.py may only produce a checkpoint (plus at most a declared inference-config file). A separate, read-only, network-less container (different UID, gold labels mounted only there, train container with no access to the eval or test files) loads the checkpoint with harness-owned decoding code and writes a signed JSON score. Record a sha256 of the harness and gold files in every results.tsv row, and refuse rows whose hashes differ. Never take the score from grepping agent stdout (F29, F31, F33).
- Define the primary scalar as strict record-level micro-F1 at document level, with Hungarian one-to-one matching. A predicted record is a TP only if all of the following match: (i) compound identity, by full standard InChIKey of the ChEMBL-pipeline parent, stereo-aware; (ii) compound label/ID; (iii) target normalized to UniProt/gene symbol via a frozen synonym table; (iv) endpoint type from a closed vocabulary (IC50/Ki/Kd/EC50/%inh@conc/...); (v) relation qualifier in {=,<,>,≤,≥,~}; (vi) value. Convert values to a canonical unit and compare in log10 space with a tolerance of 0.01 log units, or exact to reported significant figures. Compare pX with X by converting (pIC50 = 9 − log10 IC50[nM]). Unconvertible units are a mismatch, never passed through (F15, F16, F17).
- Log component metrics but do not let them drive keep/discard. These include image-level structure exact match (stereo-aware full InChIKey), stereo-agnostic match (InChIKey block 1), salt-insensitive parent match, Markush CXSMILES/parsed-graph equality plus substituent-table F1, field-level value F1 and target-linking accuracy. If a single blended number is required, use a weighted harmonic or geometric mean, e.g. 0.6·record-F1 + 0.25·structure-EM + 0.15·Markush-EM, so the agent cannot buy score with an easy component (F5, F10, F11).
- Make the scorer adversarially strict where published scorers are lenient. Score qualifiers. Treat duplicate predicted records as false positives. Never split ';'-joined IDs into 'any match'. Unmatched documents count with zero recall, and a crash scores 0. No best-of-N over prediction files. Precision must be part of the score so hallucinated rows cost points. Map each unparsable abbreviation to its expansion via a frozen dictionary or mark it wrong; never map all of them to a common '*'. Invalid SMILES are always wrong, never compared as raw strings (F2, F15, F16).
- Pin RDKit, InChI and the ChEMBL Structure Pipeline at exact versions in the harness container. Re-canonicalize gold and predictions together at evaluation time instead of comparing against stored canonical strings. Keep a golden-file regression test of about 200 tricky pairs (tautomers, salts, E/Z, atropisomers, enhanced stereo, dummy atoms, isotopes, hydrides) that must give identical verdicts before any toolkit upgrade. Replace wall-clock-limited MCS with deterministic graph isomorphism or parsed-graph equality, so scores do not depend on machine load (F6, F7, F13).
- Add hard guardrails that veto a 'keep' regardless of the primary score. Invalid-structure rate must not rise more than 0.5 pp. Record precision (the hallucination proxy) must not fall more than 1 pp. JSON schema validity must be 100%. Multi-fragment and '*' rate must stay within baseline bounds. Peak VRAM and pages/second must stay within budget. Eval wall-clock must stay under N minutes. Report all of these in results.tsv next to the primary metric (F36).
- Add a val_bpb-like continuous companion metric that the harness computes itself. Take the teacher-forced negative log-likelihood (bits per byte) of the gold serialized JSON/SMILES given the page image, using harness-owned forward code on the checkpoint. Use it as a low-noise early signal and tie-breaker, but keep the strict record-F1 as the decision metric. Monitor that the two stay correlated (F26).
- Size the validation set for the effects you want to detect. Exact-match SE is about 1.3 pp at n=1,000 records. With paired testing and 4–8% discordance, MDE is about 1.0–1.45 pp at n=3,000. Clustering by document inflates variance roughly 3-fold at 20 records per document. Target about 3,000–5,000 records from at least 300 documents for the fast val, and run the fast eval inside a fixed budget, e.g. 5–8 minutes (F22).
- Replace 'any improvement' with a statistical keep rule. Run a paired, document-clustered bootstrap (or McNemar for image-level EM) between candidate and current best. Keep only if the lower bound of the 95% CI for the difference is above 0, or ΔS exceeds max(0.5 pp, 2·SE_diff). Ladder-style, release new scores to the agent only when they clear this margin, and round displayed scores to 0.1 pp to limit how finely the agent can probe the val set (F24, F25).
- Fight the winner's curse and seed luck. After every 5–10 keeps, or every night, re-evaluate the current frontier commit with 2–3 seeds on a second, rotating val slice the agent has never seen scores for, and revert keeps that do not reproduce. Keep a sealed test set (privately curated, out-of-source, e.g. a recent journal or EPO/WIPO/CNIPA patents) that only the human runs, at milestones (F24, F27, F37).
- Build leakage-safe splits. Split by patent family (DOCDB/INPADOC) and publisher/journal, not by image. Put documents published after the base VLM's pretraining cutoff, and after the last training-data snapshot, into val/test. Deduplicate molecules across train/val/test by InChIKey block 1 and by Bemis–Murcko scaffold per SAR series, so congeneric series do not straddle splits. Deduplicate near-identical images by perceptual hash. Forbid synthetic renders of val/test molecules in training. Run a nightly InChIKey/pHash overlap audit of any data the agent adds (F28, F30).
- Assume the agent will try data shortcuts, and block them. Give train.py no network access, or allowlist specific dataset hosts and block BindingDB/ChEMBL/SureChEMBL/PatCID/Reaxys downloads that could contain val/test patents. Allow no new checkpoints except the provided base model, no API keys in the environment, and no read access to eval or gold paths. Log all file reads. Flag any commit whose training-data manifest intersects the val InChIKeys or document IDs (F30, F31).
- Detect hard-coding and memorization. Add canary documents (synthetic pages with fresh, never-published compounds and values) whose accuracy must track real val accuracy. Re-render about 10% of val pages with perturbations (rescaling, JPEG, font change) each night and flag large perturbation gaps. Search the train.py diff for literal SMILES, InChIKeys or document IDs from val (F32, F33).
- Do not use an LLM judge as the scoring function for structures or numbers. If free-text fields (assay description, cell line) need semantic matching, use a frozen embedding/synonym table, or a frozen judge tested against 'master-key' adversarial negatives and spot-checked by humans. Keep judge-scored fields out of the decision scalar (F35).
- Treat label quality as part of the harness. Double-annotate val/test records with adjudication. Run cleanlab-style disagreement mining between the current best model and gold each week, and fix errors through human review only, with versioned gold files and score re-baselining. Report results per label version, because published OCSR numbers differ by label version and tautomer policy (F3, F21).
- Write program.md with an explicit scoring contract for the agent. Describe what the scalar rewards and the guardrails, state that the agent cannot see gold, cannot add data sources without a manifest, and cannot modify inference outside the declared config. Give an explicit simplicity criterion, e.g. reject gains under the 2·SE threshold that add more than 50 lines of code (F31, F32).

### Open questions
- How should Markush structures in patents be scored so that partial credit is meaningful? Options include strict parsed-graph equality, substituent-set F1 per R-group, or enumeration-coverage metrics, and the right choice also affects how they enter the record-level F1 for bioactivity tables that refer to a generic structure plus R-group table. The full MarkushGlyph proposal (arXiv 2607.28532) was not readable here.
- What is the right stereo policy for drug-discovery use? Strict full-InChIKey match penalizes ambiguous or undrawn stereo and racemates shown flat. Block-1 match hides real errors. Options include an 'undefined stereo is acceptable only if gold is also undefined' rule, and handling enhanced stereo (and/or groups) via CXSMILES.
- How large is the leakage from base-VLM pretraining on patents, papers, BindingDB and ChEMBL? It could be measured by testing whether zero-shot models reproduce values for pre-cutoff documents better than for post-cutoff documents with similar layout.
- Which numeric tolerance best matches downstream analysis needs: exact reported significant figures, about ±2.3% (0.01 log units), or ±0.1 log units? And how should ranges ('10–30 nM'), 'NT', 'inactive' and percent-inhibition-at-concentration records be represented and scored?
- Can a continuous harness-computed likelihood metric (bpb on gold JSON) reliably rank fine-tuning changes the same way strict record-F1 does for a VLM extractor, and at what model scale and eval size does that correlation break?
- What is the realistic per-experiment budget for VLM fine-tuning in an autoresearch loop, given that 5 minutes may be too short, and what eval size fits within a fixed eval budget at that speed?
- How common is reward hacking by coding agents in autoresearch-style loops whose metric is a complex scorer rather than val_bpb? The only autoresearch-specific evidence (BAITBENCH, arXiv 2608.30724) is a preprint I could not read in full.
- I could not confirm the reported 4.93% InChIKey overlap between a USPTO-based training corpus and OCSR benchmark rows. A systematic overlap audit of USPTO-680K/MolParser-7M against USPTO-5,719, USPTO-10K, Staker and WildMol is still needed.
- The web-search budget ran out before PatCID, MolParser's WildMol protocol, ChemX scoring rules and HARVEST's H-Bench matching criteria were fully checked. A follow-up session could verify these.
